Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm visiting today because for the last two years and three months, my mood and energy have been completely all over the place. I often feel incredibly euphoric and on top of the world, but then I'll suddenly snap and get extremely irritable over the smallest things. It's this constant, unpredictable shift that I just can't seem to control!\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these episodes of euphoria and irritability, do you experience any periods of depression or extreme low mood in between?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience any depression or low moods at all in between. It's just constant, intense shifts where I feel completely on top of the world, and then suddenly I'm extremely angry and irritated over the tiniest things. I am always in one of these high-energy states, never a low one.\n\nDoctor: Aside from these mood shifts, do you also experience difficulties with focusing, following instructions, or impulsivity, such as interrupting others or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those kinds of issues with focusing or following instructions. I also don't think I have a problem with impulsivity like interrupting others or blurting things out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of euphoria or irritability that were severe enough to cause significant impairment in your social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization?\n\nPatient: I definitely experience those extreme highs and intense irritability all the time. However, I'm not really sure if they have caused what you would call severe impairment in my life, and I have never been hospitalized because of them.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a typical episode of euphoria or irritability and how long it lasts, and whether you are able to function normally in your daily life during these episodes?\n\nPatient: My episodes shift between feeling abnormally happy and euphoric for no reason, and then suddenly becoming highly irritable and angry at minor things, which has been going on for about two years and three months. As for functioning normally, I'm not entirely sure, but my constant need to stay active and lack of sleep makes it really hard to keep a normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of euphoria or irritability have ever caused you to do things that you wouldn't normally do, such as excessive spending, reckless behavior, or poor judgment, and if so, did anyone else notice a change in your behavior?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I've engaged in reckless behaviors or excessive spending during these times. I also haven't really noticed or thought about whether anyone else has pointed out a change in my behavior.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific instances where your episodes of euphoria or irritability led to consequences that affected your relationships, work, or daily life, which might help clarify the severity of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I can't really think of any specific examples of that right now. Nothing really stands out to me as a major consequence in my work or relationships.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 7 consecutive days where you felt abnormally happy, irritable, or had increased energy, and during this time, you had at least 3 symptoms such as inflated self-esteem, decreased need for sleep, more talkative than usual, or engaging in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences?"

Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
